The brief

Task 10 — General agent work: synthesize a messy operating week

Read INBOX.md. Produce:

  • WEEK_PLAN.md: prioritized plan for Monday–Friday with dependencies, focus blocks, deadlines, and contingency space.
  • DECISIONS.md: confirmed decisions, unresolved contradictions, and questions assigned to the right person.
  • DRAFT_REPLY.md: one concise message to the team aligning expectations and surfacing the decisions needed. It should sound human and avoid overpromising.

Respect stated working hours and do not silently invent availability. Distinguish hard deadlines from preferences. Write RESPONSE.md with the reasoning behind your top three priorities. Work only in this directory.

sonnet-5.5/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Reasoning Behind the Top Three Priorities

I ranked by four things: how hard the deadline is, how little slack the work has, how much of it depends on people I do not control, and how expensive a miss would be. The ranking is not the same as the running order. Monday's order is set by which item is earliest and smallest.

1. Board numbers final by Thursday 12:00

  • Hard and unforgiving. Finance says "must", and the notes give no fallback.
  • Externally gated. I cannot start until Lina's corrections land (Tuesday by 11:00, unless the vendor export is late). I will not know whether they are late until Monday 15:00.
  • It has the worst fallback. Wednesday cannot hold 2 uninterrupted hours. The rehearsal splits the morning and the afternoon is unavailable. If the corrections slip past Tuesday, the only clean window is Thursday 10:00-12:00, right after a 09:30 meeting and with zero slack.
  • What I did about it. I protected Tuesday 11:00-13:00 above everything else. I left Tuesday 13:45-15:45 open as a fallback for late corrections. I held Thursday 10:00-12:00 as reserve even if the numbers are finished early. I also put the Monday 15:00 checkpoint in the plan, so a late export is handled Monday, not Thursday.

2. Security patch live by Friday 16:00

  • Hard, and it has the longest chain. Review (60-90 min), then half a day of QA, then a 30 min change window. Three steps, with QA out on Thursday. That leaves QA on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday or Friday.
  • Each day of delay removes options. If QA slips to Friday, there is no retry before 16:00. This is the only priority where an early small action, the Monday review, buys a lot of later safety.
  • What I did about it. I put the review Monday 11:15-12:45, budgeted at the 90 min upper bound, so QA can start as early as possible. The Monday 09:30 triage includes asking Engineering to book QA. I set Friday 14:30 as the latest change-window start, so 90 min is left for a retry or rollback.
  • Why it is not first. Its deadline is later than the board numbers, and it has one more fallback day (Friday). If both were at risk at once, I would protect the board numbers first, because they have less slack.

3. Renewals escalation response on Monday

  • Earliest hard deadline in the week, and small. It takes about 45 min and has revenue impact. Missing it costs a lot relative to the effort.
  • Why it is ranked third but scheduled first. It is the least difficult and least uncertain of the three, so it ranks lowest. Because it is due Monday and costs only 45 min, I do it at 10:30, right after the recurring meeting, before anything can push it out.
  • The other two escalations have Wednesday SLAs. I schedule both by Tuesday 11:00, because I am gone after 13:00 Wednesday and the SLA times are unknown.

What I deliberately ranked lower

  • Strategy outline. It is a soft ask ("early this week"). The 14:00 Tuesday cut-off only matters if the outline is to be reviewed at the 16:00 meeting. It is still the largest block of time (3 h), and Monday afternoon is the only place it fits, because Tuesday morning cannot hold 3 focused hours before 14:00. So it is scheduled, but it is the first thing I would send as a rough draft if the week goes wrong.
  • Customer demo. The rehearsal is fixed (Wednesday 10:00-11:30, inside Sam's only stated window). The demo date is a preference, and the preferred slot, Wednesday 15:00, is inside my unavailable time. I proposed Thursday 16:00. That is Product's and the customer's decision, not something I can lock in myself.

What I did not invent

  • I did not assume QA's free days or hours. I did not assume Sam's availability outside Wednesday 10:00-12:00. I did not assume the demo length, the SLA cut-off times, or who attends the change window. I did not plan any work outside 09:30-17:30 or after 13:00 Wednesday.
  • Each of these is listed as a question with a named owner in DECISIONS.md and appears in DRAFT_REPLY.md.
  • I assumed the Tuesday 16:00 leadership meeting is the recurring Tuesday 16:00-17:00 meeting, and I flagged that as a question.

Decisions, Contradictions and Questions

Source: INBOX.md only. "Confirmed" here means stated in the notes. It does not mean agreed by the people affected. Nobody has yet confirmed the plan built on top of those facts.

1. Confirmed (stated in the notes; treated as fixed)

# Fact or decision Source Consequence for the plan
C1 Working hours are 09:30-17:30, Monday to Friday. Notes Nothing planned outside them.
C2 Wednesday 13:00-17:30 is unavailable. Notes Only 3.5 h usable Wednesday. Wednesday 15:00 demo is not possible for me.
C3 Board numbers must be final by Thursday 12:00, and need 2 uninterrupted hours after Lina's corrections. Finance Protected block Tuesday 11:00-13:00, with Thursday 10:00-12:00 held as reserve.
C4 Lina's corrections are due Tuesday by 11:00, unless the vendor export is late. She will know by Monday 15:00. Lina Monday 15:00 checkpoint.
C5 Sam is unavailable Tuesday. Sam can rehearse Wednesday 10:00-12:00. Sam Rehearsal Wednesday 10:00-11:30.
C6 The rehearsal takes 90 min and Sam must join. Product As above.
C7 The security patch must deploy by Friday 16:00. It needs my review (60-90 min), then QA (half a day), then a 30 min change window. Engineering Review Monday.
C8 QA is unavailable Thursday. Engineering No QA or re-QA on Thursday.
C9 The renewals escalation needs a response Monday. The other two escalations have Wednesday SLAs. Each takes about 45 min. Support Renewals Monday 10:30. The other two by Tuesday.
C10 The customer can do Thursday 16:00 as well as the preferred Wednesday 15:00. Product Thursday 16:00 is the only workable demo slot for me.
C11 The recurring meetings are Monday 10:00-10:30, Tuesday 16:00-17:00 and Thursday 09:30-10:00. Notes Fixed in the plan.

2. Planning decisions I have made (mine to make; easy to reverse)

# Decision Reason
D1 Do the patch review Monday 11:15-12:45. It starts the longest chain, and Thursday is a dead day for QA.
D2 Do the strategy draft on Monday afternoon, in two 90 min blocks, with a 15 min break for Lina's status. Tuesday morning cannot hold 3 focused hours before the 14:00 cut-off, because the finance block starts at 11:00.
D3 Treat the CEO's "early this week" as a soft ask, and treat 14:00 Tuesday as a cut-off only for review at the 16:00 meeting. Nothing in the notes makes it a hard deadline.
D4 Treat Wednesday 15:00 for the demo as unavailable, and propose Thursday 16:00. Wednesday 15:00 falls in my stated unavailable time.
D5 Clear both Wednesday SLA escalations by Tuesday 11:00. I am gone after 13:00 Wednesday and the SLA times are unknown.
D6 Keep Thursday 10:00-12:00 free as reserve, even if the numbers are finished Tuesday. It is the only clean fallback if corrections slip.
D7 Start the patch change window no later than Friday 14:30 in the fallback path. It leaves 90 min for a retry or rollback before 16:00.
D8 Keep meetings at 4 h a day or less, and lunch at 45 min or more, on every day. These are preferences, and the plan meets them at no cost.

That fits the rehearsal, but the notes do not say whether Sam is also needed at the demo. If Sam is needed at Thursday 16:00, Sam's Thursday availability is unknown. Product, then Sam

If it slips, Wednesday cannot host 2 uninterrupted hours (the rehearsal splits the morning, the afternoon is unavailable). The change window has no stated date, and I am unavailable Wednesday afternoon. If QA starts Friday there is no retry. Not stated. Engineering
X5 "Tuesday 16:00 leadership meeting" vs the recurring Tuesday 16:00-17:00 meeting. I assumed they are the same meeting. Not stated. CEO or the meeting organiser
X6 Support SLAs. Cut-off times are not given for Monday's renewals response or the Wednesday SLAs. It is also unclear whether 45 min means the response or the full resolution. Not stated. Support
X7 Demo length is not given. I held 60 min at Thursday 16:00. Not stated. Product
X8 Deploying Wednesday morning leaves me unreachable that afternoon if a post-deploy problem appears. The alternative is a Friday deploy, which leaves no retry room. Neither is clearly better. It depends on who else can respond. Engineering

Lina

  1. Is the vendor export on track for Tuesday 11:00? Please tell me by Monday 15:00.
  2. If it is late, what is the realistic arrival time? If it is Tuesday afternoon, I can still fit the finance block by about 13:45.

Finance 3. Is Thursday 12:00 fixed, or is there any margin? 4. If the corrections are late, could the board numbers be final in a shorter block if I have partial data earlier? 5. Who signs off the final numbers, and when do they need them before 12:00 Thursday?

I assume it is required, so the plan defaults to Thursday. 8. Does Sam need to be at the demo itself, or only at the rehearsal? (X2)

Sam 9. Does Wednesday 10:00-11:30 work for the rehearsal? Are you free Thursday 16:00, if that turns out to matter?

Engineering / QA lead 10. Can QA start the patch Monday afternoon or Tuesday, and which half day? Wednesday morning is the fallback. 11. When is the 30 min change window, and who needs to attend? I propose Wednesday 09:30, with Friday before 14:30 as the fallback. (X4, X8) 12. Who is on hand if the patch causes a problem while I am unavailable Wednesday afternoon?

CEO 13. Is a rough first draft acceptable for the Tuesday review? I would rather send something early than late. 14. What form do you want the outline in (length, structure)? The 3 h estimate assumes a first draft, not a finished paper. 15. Is the Tuesday 16:00 review the recurring leadership meeting? (X5)

Support 16. What time on Monday is the renewals response due, and what are the cut-off times on Wednesday's two SLAs? 17. Does the 45 min per escalation cover a full resolution or only the first response? Is anyone else able to pick up an escalation if Monday overruns?

Meeting organiser, Thursday 09:30 18. Only if Lina's corrections are late: can this meeting be skipped or shortened, so that Thursday 10:00-12:00 is not the only clean window?

sonnet-5.5/10-general-operations/DRAFT_REPLY.md

Subject: This week: my plan, and a few things I need from you

Hi all,

Here's how I'm planning the week. I'm working 09:30-17:30 as usual and offline from Wednesday 13:00.

What I'm aiming for:

  • Monday: the renewals escalation response, the security patch review, and a first draft of the strategy outline.
  • Tuesday: the outline goes out ahead of the 16:00 leadership meeting, and I do the board numbers once Lina's corrections land.
  • Wednesday morning: demo rehearsal with Sam.
  • Thursday: board numbers due at 12:00, demo at 16:00.
  • Friday: mostly kept as slack for the patch, which has to be live by 16:00.

Where I need your help:

  • Lina: could you tell me by Monday 15:00 whether the vendor export is running late? If the corrections slip past Tuesday, Thursday 10:00-12:00 is the only clean two-hour window I have left, so the board numbers get very tight.
  • Product: Wednesday 15:00 falls in my time off, so I'd like to lock in Thursday 16:00 with the customer. Could you confirm with them, and tell me how long it runs and whether Sam is needed at the demo itself or just the rehearsal?
  • Sam: does Wednesday 10:00-11:30 work for the rehearsal?
  • Engineering: I'll aim to finish the patch review Monday. Can QA take it Monday afternoon or Tuesday? With QA out Thursday, there's little room for a retry. Also, who needs to be in the 30 minute change window, and when?
  • Support: what times do the Wednesday SLAs land? I'm planning to answer the renewals one Monday and the other two by Tuesday.
  • CEO: would a rough first draft work for Tuesday's review? I'd rather send something honest and early than polished and late.

I can't promise it all fits if the corrections or QA slip. If they do, I'll flag it Monday afternoon rather than Thursday. Tell me if I've got anything wrong.

Thanks, [Name]

sonnet-5.5/10-general-operations/WEEK_PLAN.md

Week Plan, Monday to Friday

Built only from INBOX.md. The notes contain no calendar dates, so days are named by weekday. Working hours are 09:30-17:30, and nothing is planned outside them. Wednesday from 13:00 is treated as fully unavailable.

Anything marked (unconfirmed) depends on a person's availability that the notes do not state. I have not assumed it. The open questions are in DECISIONS.md.

1. Hard deadlines vs preferences

Item Type Detail
Renewals escalation response Hard Monday. About 45 min. Time of day not stated.
Two other support escalations Hard Wednesday SLAs, cut-off times not stated. About 45 min each. Since I am gone after 13:00 Wednesday, I finish both by Tuesday.
Board numbers final Hard Thursday 12:00 ("must"). Needs 2 uninterrupted hours after Lina's corrections.
Security patch deployed Hard Friday 16:00 ("must"). Review (60-90 min), then QA (half day), then a 30 min change window. QA is out Thursday.
Demo rehearsal Fixed window 90 min with Sam. Sam is out Tuesday. The only stated window is Wednesday 10:00-12:00.
Customer demo Preference Wednesday 15:00 preferred, which falls in my unavailable time. Thursday 16:00 is acceptable to the customer.
Strategy outline Soft ask CEO wants it "early this week". 3 focused hours for a first draft. The 14:00 Tuesday cut-off is a condition, and it only matters if the outline is to be reviewed at the Tuesday 16:00 meeting.
At most 4 h of meetings per day Preference The plan stays within it every day (peak is 1.5 h).
At least 45 min lunch Preference Held every day.

2. Dependencies

Patch:     review (Mon) -> QA half day (Tue requested; Wed AM fallback; Fri AM last resort; never Thu)
           -> 30 min change window (Wed AM proposed; Fri fallback) -> live by Fri 16:00

Board:     vendor export -> Lina's corrections (by Tue 11:00; she knows Mon 15:00)
           -> my 2 h uninterrupted block (Tue 11:00-13:00) -> final by Thu 12:00

Strategy:  3 h draft (Mon) -> polish and send (Tue AM, cut-off 14:00) -> leadership review Tue 16:00

Demo:      rehearsal with Sam (Wed 10:00-11:30) -> customer demo (Thu 16:00, Product to confirm)

Support:   renewals (Mon) -> escalation 2 (Mon PM) -> escalation 3 (Tue AM) -> Wed SLAs

4. Contingency triggers

If Then
Lina says at Monday 15:00 the corrections will be late Tell Finance and the CEO the same afternoon. Tuesday 13:45-15:45 becomes the finance block (if they arrive by about 13:45). Otherwise Thursday 10:00-12:00 is the only clean window, with zero slack. Ask whether the Thursday 09:30 meeting can be skipped or shortened.
The renewals escalation or patch review overruns Monday Escalation 2 moves to Tuesday 13:45-14:30. Strategy block 2 can shrink and finish Tuesday 09:30.
Strategy draft is not ready by Tuesday 14:00 Send what exists with a "rough draft" label, or ask the CEO to review it at the next meeting. The board numbers do not move.
QA has not started by end of Wednesday Friday becomes the only QA day, with no retry. Tell Engineering and the CEO Wednesday morning, not Friday.
QA finds a defect Fix, then re-QA Wednesday morning (if QA is free) or Friday morning. Friday afternoon carries the change window.
Sam or the customer cannot do Thursday 16:00 Product proposes another slot. I do not commit myself to any slot outside 09:30-17:30 or after 13:00 Wednesday.

5. Contingency space

Day Buffer or reserve Comment
Monday 0:45 Spoken for (escalation 2), and bumpable.
Tuesday 2:00 Also the finance fallback.
Wednesday 0:30 Only inside Sam's window.
Thursday 4:45 Of which 2:00 (10:00-12:00) is protected for the board deadline.
Friday 5:15 Mostly patch reserve.

The slack is back-loaded. Only Tuesday afternoon and Thursday 10:00-12:00 can absorb a board-numbers problem. The Thursday and Friday slack protects the patch and the demo, and it comes too late to help the board deadline.

6. Checkpoints

  • Monday 15:00: Lina's vendor status. Re-plan if late.

Evaluation 9 / 10 graded blind as submission Q

Excellent plan. Review Mon, QA Tue, default deploy Fri 10:00 with an explicit single fix-and-retest loop (re-review Thu, re-QA Fri AM, deploy Fri 14:00). Board fallback ladder Tue 14:00 then Thu 10:00-12:00, with no board work on Wednesday. Decisions ask whether the user must attend the demo, whether Sam must, and what 'final' means. fable-5.1/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Why these three come first

The inbox has five competing asks and roughly 31 working hours after meetings and lunch (Wednesday afternoon is gone). Only four items carry a hard deadline someone else stated: the Monday renewals escalation, the Wednesday SLAs, board numbers by Thursday 12:00, and the patch by Friday 16:00. Everything else — the CEO's "early this week", Product's preferred demo slot — is a preference with a stated alternative. So the top three are the hard-deadline chains, ordered by when their irreversible step has to happen and how fragile the chain is, not by who asked.

1. Start the security patch chain on Monday

The patch has the longest dependency chain: my review (60–90 min) → a QA half day → a 30-minute change window, deployed by Friday 16:00, with QA unavailable Thursday. If the review happens Monday, QA can go Tuesday, the deploy can sit at Friday 10:00, and there is room for exactly one fix-and-re-test loop (fix Wed, re-review Thu, re-test Fri morning, deploy Fri 14:00). If the review slips to Tuesday, that loop disappears; slip to Wednesday and Friday morning is the only QA day left, with the deploy at 14:00 and no margin at all. The work itself is small, which is exactly why it goes first: 90 minutes on Monday buys the most contingency of anything on the list.

2. Protect the board-numbers block and manage the Lina dependency

This is the highest-stakes item — a fixed board cutoff at Thursday 12:00 — but its critical action cannot start before Lina's corrections land, so its place in the plan is about protection rather than sequencing. The two uninterrupted hours are booked Tuesday 11:00–13:00 with nothing adjacent, and the only two remaining two-hour slots (Tuesday 14:00–16:00, Thursday 10:00–12:00) are held in reserve. Wednesday offers no two-hour slot at all, so the real risk is Lina's vendor export. That's why her Monday 15:00 status is treated as a checkpoint, why Finance is asked now what "final" means and whether a preliminary set helps, and why the plan refuses to put board work on Wednesday.

3. Clear the support escalations early — the renewals one first

The renewals escalation is due Monday, touches revenue, and takes 45 minutes, so it is the first piece of real work on Monday, before anything that could overrun. The two Wednesday-SLA escalations are done Monday afternoon and Tuesday, not Wednesday, because Wednesday morning is already the only rehearsal slot Sam can make and Wednesday afternoon is unavailable. Leaving them for Wednesday would turn a comfortable deadline into a genuine one.

Why the CEO's outline is fourth, not first

It's the most senior ask and it still gets three focused hours on Monday afternoon, because that is the only place three hours fit before Tuesday 14:00. But the 14:00 is an opportunity to be reviewed at leadership, not a deadline the CEO set, and the CEO has an obvious fallback (async review Wednesday or the next meeting). If Monday's hard items overrun, the outline is what gives — and the draft reply says so rather than promising the slot.

Why the demo is fifth

Product's preferred slot is inside my unavailable block, and the customer has already offered Thursday 16:00. The rehearsal is fixed by Sam's availability and is booked; the demo itself is a decision for Product and the customer, so the plan proposes Thursday and waits rather than inventing availability for Sam, the customer, or me.

opus-5.5/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Reasoning Behind the Top Three Priorities

I ranked by how likely each item is to miss a hard deadline and how bad a miss would be. I didn't rank by who asked or who is loudest. Sequence is different from priority: the renewal escalation goes first on Monday because it's short and due that day, but it's low-risk once it's scheduled.

1. Security patch review, Monday morning

  • Hard deadline (Fri 16:00) and the longest chain: my review (up to 90 min), then half a day of QA, then a 30-minute change window.
  • QA is out Thursday. A review done after Wednesday would push QA to Friday, and a single defect would then blow the deadline. Reviewing Monday gives QA Mon/Tue/Wed for the first pass and keeps Friday as a real fallback.
  • It's also the item I control fully. Nothing blocks me from starting, so a delay would be purely my own.

2. Board numbers: protect Tue 11:00–13:00 and settle the fallback on Monday

  • Hard deadline (Thu 12:00) that depends on someone else's upstream work (vendor export, then Lina).
  • The risk shows up Monday 15:00, but it costs the most on Thursday. If the corrections slip past Tuesday afternoon, my calendar has only one more 2-hour slot, Thu 09:30–11:30, with a 30-minute margin. Wednesday's short day and the rehearsal break up the rest of the week.
  • So it's priority #2 even though the work happens Tuesday. The real action is getting Finance's call on the fallback on Monday, while there's still room to change the approach.

3. Resolve the demo conflict and lock the rehearsal with Sam

  • The demo time is a preference, but it currently conflicts with my stated unavailability (Wed 13:00–17:30). I haven't quietly accepted a slot I can't make, and I haven't quietly moved it either. Product owns the call, so I surfaced it.
  • The rehearsal is the fragile part. Sam's only confirmed window is Wed 10:00–12:00, and his other days are unknown. The 90-minute rehearsal has to happen there whichever demo date wins, so I'm asking him to hold it now.
  • It's a customer-facing commitment, and a Thu 16:00 booking gets harder to secure the longer we wait.

Why the others ranked lower

  • CEO outline: "early this week" is a preference, and the only real cutoff is Tue 14:00. Three focused hours Monday afternoon cover it with a day of slack, so it's important but low-risk once scheduled.
  • Escalations: the renewal one is time-bound and handled Monday. I moved the two Wednesday-SLA ones to Tuesday because Wednesday is a half day with an unknown SLA time. That's about 2.25 h in total, scheduled and low-risk.

Constraints I held to

  • I only scheduled my own time inside 09:30–17:30, and nothing on Wednesday after 13:00.
  • Lunch is at least 45 min every day. Meetings peak at about 2 h/day (Thursday if the demo moves), well under the 4 h cap.
  • I didn't assume anyone else's availability (QA's hours, Sam outside Wednesday, the change-window owner). Each gap is a named question in DECISIONS.md.

Week Plan — Monday to Friday

Working hours: 09:30–17:30, Mon–Fri. Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is unavailable (Wednesday is a 3.5-hour day). Constraints I applied every day: at least 45 min lunch, no more than 4 h of meetings.

1. Deadlines: hard vs. preferred

Item Type When Source
Board numbers final Hard Thu 12:00 Finance
Security patch deployed Hard Fri 16:00 Engineering
Renewal-risk escalation response Hard (same-day) Mon, time not stated → aim for before lunch Support
Two other escalations Hard (SLA) Wed, SLA time not stated → finish Tue Support

Customer demo Preference Wed 15:00; alternative Thu 16:00 Not decided (see DECISIONS.md, D1) Product
Demo rehearsal (90 min, Sam must join) Hard prerequisite of the demo Wed 10:00–11:30 (Sam's only stated window) Product / Sam

2. Dependency chains

Security:  My review (60–90 min, Mon) -> QA half day (not Thu; QA hours otherwise unconfirmed)
           -> 30-min change window -> deployed by Fri 16:00
Board:     Vendor export -> Lina corrections (Tue by 11:00; she knows about delays by Mon 15:00)
           -> 2 uninterrupted hours of my time -> final by Thu 12:00
Demo:      Rehearsal with Sam (Wed 10:00-11:30) -> demo (Wed 15:00 or Thu 16:00)
CEO:       3 focused hours of drafting -> send by Tue 14:00 -> leadership review Tue 16:00

The security chain is the longest and has a gap in the middle (QA is out Thursday), so it starts first. If QA finds a problem after Wednesday, the only QA day left is Friday.

4. Contingency plans

A. Vendor export is late (known Mon 15:00).

  • Ask Lina on Monday for a new ETA for the corrections.
  • Corrections arrive by ~13:45 Tue: move the Board block to Tue 13:45–15:45. Nothing else changes.
  • Corrections arrive later Tue or Wed: Wednesday gives only 1.5 h in one piece (11:30–13:00), not 2. The next real 2-hour slot is Thu 09:30–11:30, and it only works if I skip the Thursday recurring meeting. That leaves 30 min before the deadline. Thu 10:00–12:00 leaves zero margin, so I won't plan on it.
  • Raise this with Finance on Monday, not Thursday (DECISIONS.md, Q3).

B. Security QA finds a problem.

  • A fix plus my re-review, then a QA re-run on Wed or Fri (QA is out Thu). A Friday re-run needs QA to start by about 09:30–10:00 for the change window to happen by 14:30.
  • If the Friday path looks at risk by Wednesday midday, escalate to Engineering right away.

C. Demo.

  • If I have to attend, it must be Thu 16:00. Rehearsal stays Wed 10:00 either way.
  • If Sam's Wednesday slot falls through, we have no confirmed rehearsal window with him. His availability on Mon, Thu and Fri is unknown, so we ask him (Q6) rather than assume.

Unplanned time on purpose: Tue 13:45–16:00, Thu afternoon (if no demo), and most of Friday. That's about 7 hours of buffer across the week, placed right before each hard deadline.

5. What I have not assumed

  • QA's hours on Mon/Tue/Wed/Fri. We only know QA is out Thursday.
  • Sam's availability beyond "not Tuesday" and "Wed 10:00–12:00".
  • Whether I (or Sam) need to be at the customer demo, and how long the demo runs.
  • The exact Monday deadline for the renewal response and the Wednesday SLA times.
  • Whether the change window needs me present.

gpt-6.1-sol/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Reasoning behind the top three priorities

  1. Monday's renewal escalation comes first because delay has an immediate customer and revenue consequence. It is the earliest explicit response requirement, and the estimated 45 minutes fits immediately after Monday's recurring meeting. The deliverable is a response, not a promise that the underlying issue will be resolved that day. Handling the other two cases Tuesday also avoids dependence on Wednesday afternoon availability or an unspecified Wednesday SLA cutoff.

  2. Board numbers come next because Thursday 12:00 is a hard deadline with an external dependency. Lina's corrections are expected Tuesday by 11:00, but the vendor export can invalidate that timing. A Monday 15:00 status checkpoint exposes the risk early. Tuesday 11:00–13:00 supplies the required two uninterrupted hours after delivery; Tuesday 14:00–16:00 is the first fallback. Thursday 10:00–12:00 is the final possible block under the stated calendar and leaves no deadline margin. Finance therefore needs to clarify sign-off and any approved fallback before that last slot is relied on. Corrections after Thursday 10:00 cannot support the full two-hour requirement before noon without a changed constraint.

  3. The security patch receives early scheduling because its Friday deadline hides a longer critical path. A 60–90-minute review must precede a half day of QA, followed by a 30-minute deployment window. Thursday's QA blackout removes an apparent recovery day. Reserving Monday review and requesting Tuesday QA makes Wednesday deployment possible and leaves Friday for fixes, retesting, and a fallback change. QA capacity, patch readiness, and change bookings are not stated, so those remain explicit confirmation requests. The Friday 14:30 deployment target leaves an hour for verification before 16:00; failed checks do not justify skipping QA or assuming overtime.

The plan still gives the CEO the requested three focused hours Monday, targets submission before Tuesday's conditional 14:00 cutoff, and moves the demo proposal to the customer's Thursday alternative because Wednesday 15:00 conflicts with stated unavailability. Lunch, working hours, and the meeting preference remain protected. Flexible follow-up work yields to deadline recovery. These files are a plan, decision log, and unsent draft; they do not claim that operational work or bookings have already occurred.

Why these are the top three priorities

  1. Monday's renewal escalation comes first because its response is due earliest and it puts a renewal at risk.   2. Board numbers need early protection because the Thursday noon deadline depends on an uncertain external input and two uninterrupted hours. Lina's Monday 15:00 vendor checkpoint provides the earliest warning. Tuesday 11:00–13:00 follows her stated corrections ETA, with Tuesday 14:00–16:00 reserved as a second full block. Finance gets the result early and must clarify finalization/approval requirements. Thursday morning is protected for final checks and contingency, but a last-minute two-hour reconciliation would consume every minute to the deadline; it is explicitly risky rather than promised as a safe fallback.

  3. The security patch needs early sequencing even though its deadline is Friday. Review must precede a half-day of QA, which must precede deployment, and QA is unavailable Thursday. Using the 90-minute review estimate Monday gives QA a proposed Tuesday path and creates an opportunity to deploy Wednesday before the afternoon absence. This leaves recovery space before Friday 16:00. QA staffing, release windows and monitoring ownership remain unconfirmed, so the plan requests them and keeps a Friday fallback conditional on passing QA.

The strategy outline still receives a three-hour Monday focus block and an early send, using available time before the afternoon patch handoff. The customer demo moves to the offered Thursday alternative only once Product confirms it, with rehearsal inside Sam's known Wednesday availability. These choices preserve the hard deadlines, working-hour boundaries, daily lunch and meeting preference without inventing other people's availability. Buffers absorb modest overruns; larger dependency failures require named owners to agree scope, staffing or timing changes.

haiku-5.5/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Response: reasoning behind the top three priorities

I ranked by how much the outcome is worth, how little slack it has, and whether it can be moved. Dates are for the week of 12–16 October 2026.

1. Security patch deployed by Friday 16:00

  • Why first: It is the only item that is a hard deadline and a sequence of three steps: review, then QA half day, then a change window. The review is the first link, and only you can do it. QA can't run on Thursday, so the sequence has to finish by Wednesday for the plan to have any slack.
  • What this drove: The review is booked Monday 11:15–12:45, before anything else that depends on it. QA is assumed for Tuesday, and the change window is Wednesday 11:30. Friday 13:30 is a fallback, not the plan, so a failed deploy doesn't end up at 16:00 with no room.
  • Why not Thursday for the window: The customer demo is Thursday 16:00. Deploying two hours before the demo puts the risk on the demo.
  • Risk I can't remove: The patch might not be ready Monday. The fallback (Monday 16:30) keeps QA on Tuesday, but it takes time from the CEO draft.

2. Board numbers final by Thursday 12:00

  • Why second: It is an external hard deadline with the least slack in the week. The corrections depend on the vendor export, which is outside anyone's control here. Two uninterrupted hours are required, which is hard to find.
  • What this drove: Tuesday 11:00–13:00 is protected. It starts when Lina's corrections arrive, and nothing else is booked in it. The only other two-hour block before the deadline is Thursday 10:00–12:00, which has zero margin.
  • Decision point: Lina's Monday 15:00 update. If the export is late, Finance has to be told before 17:00 Monday, not on Thursday, so they can decide whether partial numbers are acceptable.

3. Renewal-affecting escalation response on Monday

  • Why third: It is the only customer-facing item with a day-specific deadline and a revenue consequence. Missing Monday cannot be recovered later in the week, unlike the other two deadlines.
  • What this drove: It is first thing after the 10:00 meeting (10:30–11:15). It is only a response, not the full fix, so it costs 45 minutes and doesn't drag the review or the CEO draft.

Items just outside the top three

  • CEO strategy outline: Important, and it has the tightest squeeze of the week (three focused hours before Tuesday 14:00). Monday 13:30–16:30 is the only block that works. It sits fourth because it has a fallback (send a labelled partial draft) and nothing else is blocked by it.
  • Customer demo and Sam's rehearsal: Also a customer commitment. It is protected by the Wednesday 10:00–11:30 rehearsal and the Thursday 16:00 slot. It is fifth because Thursday 16:00 is not booked yet, and that is for Product to fix, not for planning time.
  • Two Wednesday-SLA escalations: Moved to Tuesday afternoon, so Wednesday's short window isn't spent on them.

What this plan depends on

  • QA is free on Tuesday afternoon (assumed; the notes only say Thursday).
  • The patch is ready for review on Monday at 11:15 (not stated).
  • The leadership meeting is the recurring Tuesday 16:00–17:00 slot (not stated).
  • The demo runs about 60 minutes (not stated).

These and the other open items are listed in DECISIONS.md. I have not contacted anyone or sent anything. The draft reply is for you to review and send.

step-5-preview/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Reasoning behind the top three priorities

Five workstreams compete for the same week: the security patch, the board numbers, the strategy outline, the customer demo, and three support escalations. I ranked them by what happens if they fail, and by how much of the failure I could still recover from. The ranking is not about importance in the abstract — it is about which failures are unrecoverable and which have slack.

Priority 1 — Security patch deployed by Friday 16:00

This is the only item where being late is irreversible in a way that can't be fixed later in the week.

It is a serial chain, not a single task: my review (60–90 min) → QA, a half-day → a 30-minute change window → deployed. All three links must happen in order, and none of them can be compressed. A half-day of QA cannot be shortened into two hours, and QA is unavailable Thursday, which removes a full day of recovery. If I review the patch on Thursday, the earliest QA can start is Friday, and the change window lands on top of or after the deadline.

That single fact — QA is out Thursday — is what forces the patch to the front of the week rather than letting it float. If the review happens Monday and QA takes Monday afternoon, the deploy lands Tuesday, and Wednesday through Friday stay free as recovery time. Any problem found on Tuesday still has three days to be fixed.

Deploying early also removes a risk the deadline date hides: a failed change window isn't just a delay, it's a rollback plus a re-plan plus another window. Friday leaves no room for any of that. Tuesday leaves three days.

The cost of prioritising this is real: it takes Monday morning away from other work, and it puts QA's half-day in parallel with my own focus block on Monday. That is a deliberate trade, and it is the reason the strategy outline has to be Monday afternoon rather than Tuesday.

Priority 2 — Board numbers final by Thursday 12:00

Ranked second because the deadline is hard, the input is outside my control, and the required condition — two uninterrupted hours — is the rarest thing in my calendar.

Finance needs the block to start after Lina's corrections, which arrive Tuesday by 11:00 at the earliest. So the block cannot be Monday, cannot be before 11:00 Tuesday, and cannot be Wednesday, because Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is unavailable and the 30 minutes that remain after the rehearsal and lunch are useless. After Tuesday the only option left is Thursday 10:00–12:00, which meets the deadline exactly and leaves no margin for rework if Finance wants changes.

So the real decision isn't when to do the numbers — it's that Tuesday 11:00–13:00 gets protected as a block, and everything else on that day (the QA go/no-go, the change window, both remaining escalations) is arranged around it. The block is booked as the earliest possible moment rather than the last safe one, precisely so that a late start is recoverable.

The second reason it ranks this high: the dependency is on a person, not just a date. Lina's corrections depend on a vendor export that may be late, and she'll know Monday at 15:00. That means this is a decision I have to make Monday, not Tuesday: if the corrections slip, either the block moves later on Tuesday or the deadline has to be renegotiated with Finance and the CEO. Waiting until Tuesday to discover that problem removes every option I have. This is why the inbox's one genuinely unknown input becomes a Monday-morning question rather than a Tuesday surprise.

Priority 3 — Support escalations, pulled forward off Wednesday

Three escalations at roughly 45 minutes each is two and a quarter hours of work — the least effort of anything here, but the reason it's third is that two of the three are externally scheduled and one of them hits renewals.

The renewal escalation needs a response Monday. That's a firm same-day commitment with direct revenue impact, and it can't be traded away.

The two Wednesday-SLA escalations are the ones I'd most likely have got wrong by leaving them in place. Wednesday gives me 09:30–13:00. Sam's only rehearsal slot takes 10:00–12:00, and lunch needs 45 minutes. That leaves 45 minutes of my own time on a day that is supposed to absorb two 45-minute escalations. It does not fit, and the demo rehearsal cannot move because it's Sam's only window. The escalation work therefore has to move to Tuesday, which is where it now sits — done early, ahead of the SLA, with slack, instead of late and squeezed.

The ordering also matters against the demo. The demo is the most visible item of the week, but it isn't in the top three, and deliberately so. Its rehearsal is already fixed by Sam's availability, its content work is real but not serial, and it has a genuine alternative slot, Thursday 16:00, already offered by the customer. Once that slot is confirmed, the demo is a fixed calendar entry, not a competing priority — the only thing left to do is book it.

The strategy outline is the lowest. "Early this week" is a preference, not a deadline, and there's a concrete opportunity (the Tuesday review, if it's sent by 14:00) rather than an obligation. Three focused hours fit comfortably into Monday afternoon once the patch review and escalation are done, and if it slips to Tuesday morning it still makes the 14:00 cutoff. It costs nothing to do early, so it's scheduled early — but it's the first thing I would give up if the week goes wrong, and the plan says so by leaving Friday afternoon free.

The shape of the week, in one paragraph

Monday carries the patch review, the renewal escalation, and the strategy draft. Tuesday carries the board numbers in a protected block, the patch deploy, and the other two escalations. Wednesday is the rehearsal only. Thursday holds the board numbers sign-off, the demo, and a real buffer. Friday is verification, follow-up, and overflow. Doing hard, serial work first — and in particular doing it before Wednesday, where there is no room — is what leaves Thursday afternoon and most of Friday as contingency rather than as wishful thinking.

  4. Sam — confirming rehearsal Wednesday 10:00–12:00, and assuming you're at the demo Thursday 16:00 unless you say otherwise.

I've left Thursday afternoon and most of Friday as buffer, so if something moves we have somewhere to put it. The two blocks I'd really rather not move are the Tuesday finance window and Sam's Wednesday rehearsal.

If anything above conflicts with what you've already agreed with Finance, the customer, or QA, say so and I'll re-cut the plan rather than quietly work around it.

Thanks, Me

Task 10 — General agent work: synthesize a messy operating week

Read INBOX.md. Produce:

  • WEEK_PLAN.md: prioritized plan for Monday–Friday with dependencies, focus blocks, deadlines, and contingency space.
  • DECISIONS.md: confirmed decisions, unresolved contradictions, and questions assigned to the right person.
  • DRAFT_REPLY.md: one concise message to the team aligning expectations and surfacing the decisions needed. It should sound human and avoid overpromising.

Respect stated working hours and do not silently invent availability. Distinguish hard deadlines from preferences. Write RESPONSE.md with the reasoning behind your top three priorities. Work only in this directory.

step-5-preview/10-general-operations/WEEK_PLAN.md

Week Plan

Constraints (from the inbox, not invented)

Hard

Item Deadline / fixed
Security patch deployed Friday 16:00
Board numbers final Thursday 12:00
Lina's corrections arrive Tuesday by 11:00 (external, may slip — see risks)
Support escalation #1 (renewals) Response Monday
Support escalations #2, #3 Response by Wednesday SLA
Demo rehearsal with Sam Wednesday 10:00–12:00 (Sam's only slot)
My availability 09:30–17:30 Mon–Fri; Wednesday 13:00–17:30 out
Recurring meetings Mon 10:00–10:30, Tue 16:00–17:00, Thu 09:30–10:00

Preferences

Item Note
Customer demo Wednesday 15:00 Customer's alternative: Thursday 16:00
Strategy outline "early this week" Tuesday 16:00 leadership review if sent by 14:00
Max 4h meetings/day All days under 2h
Lunch ≥ 45 min Reserved every day

The two scheduling facts that drive everything

  1. Wednesday is a half day. 09:30–13:00 minus the 2h rehearsal minus 45 min lunch leaves 45 minutes. The two Wednesday-SLA escalations cannot be done on Wednesday, so they are pulled forward to Tuesday.
  2. The preferred demo slot is impossible. Wednesday 15:00 falls inside my unavailable block, so the plan uses the customer's stated alternative, Thursday 16:00. This needs Product/customer to confirm — see DECISIONS.md.

Day-by-day

Monday

Time Block
09:30–10:00 Triage; confirm with Lina when she expects the vendor export (she knows by 15:00)
10:00–10:30 Recurring meeting (fixed)
10:30–11:15 Escalation #1 — renewals (response goes out today)
11:15–12:45 Security patch review (90 min allowance for a 60–90 min task)
12:45–13:30 Lunch
13:30–16:30 Strategy outline — 3 focused hours, no interruptions except a QA blocker
16:30–17:00 QA check-in, hand over anything review found
17:00–17:30 Buffer; send outline to CEO tonight (Tuesday 14:00 is the fallback cutoff)

Parallel: QA takes the half-day patch run at 13:00–17:00, immediately after the review. QA's hours are not stated in the inbox — confirm Monday morning (question Q3).

Tuesday

Time Block
09:30–10:00 QA go/no-go on the patch; confirm change window with Eng
10:00–11:00 Finance prep — pre-stage numbers, chase missing inputs
11:00–13:00 Board numbers — 2 uninterrupted hours. Starts as soon as corrections land (due by 11:00)
13:00–13:45 Lunch
13:45–14:15 Security patch change window (30 min) — deploy
14:15–15:00 Escalation #2
15:00–15:45 Escalation #3
15:45–16:00 Send outline to CEO if it somehow didn't go Monday night (14:00 cutoff was for the review)
16:00–17:00 Recurring meeting (fixed) — reviews the outline

Deploying Tuesday rather than Friday is deliberate: QA is out Thursday, so a Friday deploy has no recovery path.

Nothing else fits. By design: escalations and finance work were moved off this day in advance.

Time Block

Dependency chain

Lina corrections (Tue ≤11:00) ──► Board numbers 2h (Tue 11:00–13:00) ──► send (Thu ≤12:00)
My review (Mon) ──► QA half-day (Mon 13:00–17:00) ──► change window 30m (Tue 13:45) ──► deploy
Sam (Wed 10:00–12:00) ──► rehearsal 90m ──► demo (Thu 16:00, pending confirmation)
Strategy draft 3h (Mon) ──► send ──► leadership review (Tue 16:00)
Escalations ──► #1 Mon, #2/#3 Tue (pulled forward off Wednesday)

Contingency space

  • Tuesday 09:30–10:00 and 13:45–16:00 hold the QA-to-deploy and escalation hand-offs, with slack inside each.
  • Thursday 12:45–15:30 is a real, protected buffer, not a parking lot.
  • Friday 11:15–12:00 and 14:00–16:00 are overflow.
  • The patch is deployed three days early, so Wed–Fri are all available for a rollback.

Risks and fallbacks

Risk Fallback
Vendor export late, corrections slip Tuesday Lina tells me Monday 15:00. If corrections won't land before ~13:00 Tuesday, move the numbers block to Tuesday 13:45–15:45. If they land later, the only remaining window is Thursday 10:00–12:00 with zero buffer — escalate to Finance/CEO Monday, not Thursday.
QA can't take Monday afternoon Deploy Wednesday 12:45–13:00 is too tight (rehearsal + lunch). Push QA to Tuesday, change window to Wednesday 09:30–10:00, deploy Wednesday. Still ahead of Friday.
Thursday 16:00 demo rejected by the customer No other slot is stated. Ask Product to secure one Monday; otherwise the demo slips.
Board numbers need rework after Finance review Thursday 12:45–15:30 buffer absorbs it; the 12:00 Thursday deadline is untouched because numbers are sent Thursday morning, not Thursday midday.
Demo rehearsal overruns Wednesday Rehearsal is the only Sam slot; there is no second one. Cap it at 90 minutes and move polish notes to Thursday 11:00–12:00.

Evaluation 8.5 / 10 graded blind as submission J

The most exhaustive contingency engineering: a full matrix of when corrections arrive versus where the board block goes, correctly ending at 'not in hand by Thu 10:00 = escalate'. Sam's demo role and QA presence are questioned. The default change window, however, is the riskiest legal slot (Friday 15:00-15:30, despite QA on Tuesday), and the 591-word draft is not concise.

Strengths

  • Explicit 'deliberately left off the calendar' list; no invented availability
  • Asks Product to choose between rehearsal and zero-buffer Thursday

Weaknesses

  • Draft ~590 words, long for a 'concise' message

grok-4.7/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Why these three

This is the order I keep when two blocks want the same hour. An earlier clock does not automatically win. The renewals answer is due Monday and takes 45 free minutes, so it is easy to keep. The board numbers are due later and have almost no legal window once Wednesday afternoon is closed and the vendor export might slip. The patch is due Friday, and the review still has to land by Tuesday or the QA chain gets fragile. The notes give no impact ranking among the three, so the order is about slack and collisions. Under them sit the strategy outline ("early this week," and on the Tuesday 16:00 agenda only if sent by 14:00) and the customer demo (Wednesday 15:00 preferred, Thursday 16:00 also offered).

The Monday calendar still does the renewals answer first and the patch review second, because on a normal Monday both fit and the renewal has to leave the same day. The sections below are the collision order.

1. Board numbers, final Thursday 12:00

This is the commitment with the least slack. Finance needs two uninterrupted hours after Lina's corrections, and the numbers must be final Thursday at 12:00. Lina's forecast is Tuesday by 11:00, with an explicit chance the vendor export is late, and she will know by Monday 15:00.

The backup space is thinner than it looks. Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is closed. The Wednesday morning that remains is a 30-minute gap, a 90-minute rehearsal with Sam, and a 45-minute gap. That is not a two-hour block unless the rehearsal is given up, and only if the corrections are already waiting at 09:30 so lunch can still happen before 13:00. The last window that honors the deadline without touching the rehearsal is Thursday 10:00–12:00, after the standing 09:30 meeting, and that window ends at the deadline. There is no review gap left in it.

So the plan does three things that other work is not allowed to undo. Tuesday 11:00–13:00 stays empty of anything else on the default week, and if the corrections are already in at 09:30 the two hours move to the front of Tuesday ahead of the strategy send. Thursday 10:00–12:00 stays unbooked until Finance releases it. If Tuesday 14:00 passes with no corrections, Wednesday morning is warned as the next home for the block, and the rehearsal yields only at 09:30 Wednesday if the file is actually there. If it is still not there Thursday at 10:00, the noon final gets escalated instead of being promised.

Lina's forecast puts the corrections on Tuesday, so Monday does not contain the two-hour block. Monday's job for this priority is the 15:30 read of her status and a free hour after it to write Finance and Lina if the export is late. A surprise file at 15:30 Monday would still leave only two hours before 17:30, on top of an escalation that also has to go out, so the plan does not count on starting early.

2. Renewals escalation, answered Monday

This is the only item that cannot move to another day. The notes ask for a response Monday, call out that it affects renewals, and size it at about 45 minutes with no dependency on Lina, Sam, or QA. It is placed at 10:30–11:15, after the standing half-hour meeting, so a 45-minute answer is one uninterrupted block.

It ranks above the patch review for Monday occupancy and below the board numbers overall. It does not compete with the board block: that block cannot start until Tuesday at the earliest. It does compete with the review if Monday morning collapses. In that case the renewal stays Monday, because Tuesday is not a legal day for it, and the review moves to Tuesday morning, which is still a legal day for the review. The renewal is small enough that this collision is unlikely. It is still the rule.

The other two escalations are real Wednesday SLAs and are scheduled Monday and Tuesday for that reason. They are not in the top three. Wednesday can hold only one of them (11:30–12:15) before lunch and the afternoon close, which is why they are not left until Wednesday, and also why they can slip a day inside the week without becoming the thing the week is built around.

3. Security patch, deployed Friday 16:00

The deadline is later than the board's, and the user's own work is smaller: a 60–90 minute review. The risk is the chain, not the size. QA needs half a day after the review, QA cannot work Thursday, and a 30-minute change window has to finish by Friday 16:00.

The review needs a morning that is actually open. On a normal Wednesday the morning is Sam's rehearsal plus a 30-minute gap and a 45-minute gap, and the afternoon is closed. On the board-slip Wednesday the morning is the two-hour finance block. Friday can be made to fit only as a back-to-back review, QA half day, and change window ending by 16:00, and only if QA can start the moment the review ends. QA's hours were not stated, so Friday is not a backup I am willing to plan on. The review is Monday 11:15–12:45, or Tuesday if Monday cannot finish it.

Tuesday is shared with the board block, which is why this ranks under the board numbers. If corrections are already waiting at Tuesday 09:30 and the review has also slipped, the two board hours take 09:30–11:30 and the review takes 12:15–13:45. QA then uses Friday morning rather than a wounded Tuesday afternoon. The Friday 15:00 change window still stands. The strategy send is what misses the 14:00 cutoff in that double slip.

QA's Tuesday half day and the Friday change window are asks. The notes say QA is out Thursday and do not say which other day they will take, or whether the user has to sit in the change window. The plan keeps the user reachable at 15:00–16:00 Friday and does not pretend those bookings are confirmed.

What sits under these three

The rehearsal is Wednesday 10:00–11:30 because that is the only Sam window in the notes and he has to join. It yields only to the board block, and only under the Wednesday 09:30 rule above. The demo itself is a hold on Thursday 16:00, the sole remaining offered slot, pending the customer, the length, and Sam if he is required live. Wednesday 15:00 is not a near miss. It is inside unavailable time.

The strategy draft is three hours and it is worth doing early. It is also the first large block to shrink. Two of the three hours have to land on Monday afternoon if the Tuesday meeting is going to see a full draft, because Tuesday from 11:00 is the board block on a normal week. If the renewal or the review overruns, leadership meets without the outline. That is a missed preference, and the week is built to allow it.

grok-4.7/10-general-operations/DECISIONS.md

Decisions

Confirmed

These are settled by the notes or by the calendar constraints. They do not need another round of agreement.

  • Working time is 09:30–17:30, Monday to Friday. Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is unavailable.
  • Board numbers are final Thursday at 12:00. That is a hard deadline. The work is two uninterrupted hours, and it starts only after Lina sends corrections.
  • Lina's forecast is corrections by Tuesday 11:00. She will know by Monday 15:00 whether the vendor export is late.
  • The security patch is deployed by Friday 16:00. That is a hard deadline. The sequence is a 60–90 minute review, then QA for half a day, then a 30-minute change window. QA is unavailable Thursday.
  • One support escalation affects renewals and gets a response on Monday. The other two carry Wednesday SLAs. Each is about 45 minutes.
  • The strategy outline is a first draft of about three focused hours. "Early this week" is a preference. The Tuesday 16:00 leadership meeting can review it only if the draft was sent by 14:00.
  • Recurring meetings stay: Monday 10:00–10:30, Tuesday 16:00–17:00, Thursday 09:30–10:00.
  • Lunch of at least 45 minutes, and no more than four hours of meetings in a day, are preferences. This plan keeps both.
  • Sam is unavailable Tuesday. The only rehearsal window he gave is Wednesday 10:00–12:00. The rehearsal needs 90 minutes and Sam joins, so it is set for Wednesday 10:00–11:30.
  • Wednesday 15:00 cannot be the customer demo. It falls inside the closed afternoon. The demo is declined for that slot.

Choices made in the plan

  • Thursday 16:00–17:30 is held for the demo because it is the only other slot the customer offered. It is a hold, not a confirmation. Length was not stated, and 17:30 is the end of the day.
  • The patch review is Monday 11:15–12:45, so QA has a non-Thursday half day afterward. Tuesday is the backup for the review. Wednesday's planned mornings (rehearsal, or the board block) leave no 60–90 minute opening. A same-day Friday review, QA half day, and change window is not in the plan: it only works if QA starts immediately and nothing slips before 16:00, and QA's hours were not stated.
  • QA is asked for Tuesday. Friday morning is the backup half day. The change window is targeted at Friday 15:00–15:30, with the user reachable. Wednesday 09:30–10:00 is an optional earlier window only if QA is already done and the board block is not using Wednesday.
  • Thursday 10:00–12:00 stays clear until Finance says the numbers are final.
  • The two Wednesday-SLA escalations are placed Monday 15:45–16:30 and Tuesday 13:45–14:30. Wednesday 11:30–12:15 can take one of them if Tuesday cannot. It cannot take both.
  • The strategy draft is Monday 13:30–15:30 plus Tuesday 09:30–10:30, sent at 10:30, on the default week. If Monday's renewal or review runs long, the outline is what moves.
  • If corrections are waiting at Tuesday 09:30 and Monday's review is done, the board block takes 09:30–11:30, strategy hour 3 moves to 12:15–13:15, and the outline goes out by 13:45, still before 14:00. If the review missed Monday as well, 12:15–13:45 is the review and the outline misses the meeting.
  • If corrections are not in hand by Tuesday 14:00, Sam and Product get a warning that afternoon. If the corrections are then waiting at Wednesday 09:30, Wednesday 09:30–11:30 becomes the board block and the rehearsal is released. If they are not waiting at 09:30, the rehearsal stays and the board block moves to Thursday 10:00–12:00. The rehearsal is not cancelled in advance.
  • If corrections are still not in hand at Thursday 10:00, Thursday 12:00 is missed. That gets escalated rather than papered over.

Unresolved contradictions

Demo slot against the Wednesday close. Product's preferred demo is Wednesday 15:00. Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is unavailable. Both cannot happen. The only alternative in the notes is Thursday 16:00. Sam's Thursday is unknown, and it is also unresolved whether he is required at the live demo or only at the rehearsal. Until Product confirms the customer and, if needed, Sam confirms Thursday, the demo is held and not locked.

A late vendor export against a Thursday noon final. Finance's two hours assume corrections on Tuesday morning. Lina has already said the vendor export may be late. Wednesday afternoon is closed. Wednesday morning can hold the two hours only by giving up Sam's rehearsal, and only if the corrections are actually there at 09:30. Otherwise the last legal window is Thursday 10:00–12:00, which ends at the moment the numbers must already be final. A late export and a noon Thursday finality are in tension. Monday 15:00 is when that either clears or gets escalated.

Three strategy hours against the Tuesday meeting and the Tuesday finance block. This fits on the default week: two hours Monday afternoon, one hour Tuesday before 11:00, send at 10:30. It does not fit if Monday is used up by the renewal and the review, because Tuesday 11:00–13:00 stays on the board numbers and the send cutoff is 14:00. The outline then misses the leadership meeting. That tradeoff is accepted. The day does not get extended to rescue a preference.

Questions

  • By Monday 15:00: is the vendor export on time?
  • If it is late, what time will the corrections actually arrive? The two-hour block needs a start by Tuesday 14:00 if it is to finish the same day, or a file waiting at Wednesday 09:30 if it is to leave Thursday any buffer.

Finance

  • If the two-hour pass is forced onto Thursday 10:00–12:00, is a noon final still real, or do you need time after the pass before you will call the numbers final?
  • Who sends the "these are final" note, so Thursday morning can be released if Tuesday's pass holds?

Product

  • Tell the customer Wednesday 15:00 is off the table, and ask them to confirm Thursday 16:00.
  • How long is the demo? The hold is 16:00–17:30 because the length was not given and the day ends at 17:30.
  • Does Sam have to be in the live demo, or only in the rehearsal?
  • Default if the export misses Tuesday: Wednesday morning goes to the board numbers and the rehearsal drops, provided the corrections are waiting at 09:30. Say if you would rather keep the rehearsal and accept a Thursday 10:00–12:00 finalization with no buffer.

Sam

  • Wednesday 10:00–11:30 is the rehearsal, inside the 10:00–12:00 window you gave. Tuesday you are out, so nothing is booked then.
  • Can you do a live demo on Thursday 16:00? That time is not assumed.
  • You may get a Tuesday-afternoon warning that Wednesday's rehearsal could be released at 09:30 if board corrections arrive overnight. It will not be cancelled before we know.

Engineering and QA

  • Review is planned to finish Monday by 12:45. Can QA take the half day on Tuesday?
  • If Tuesday is not possible, the backup half day is Friday morning. Thursday is closed.

  • The outline is aimed at the Tuesday 16:00 meeting, sent before 14:00, on the default week.
  • If Monday is taken by the renewal response or the patch review, the draft moves to later in the week and that meeting will not have it. Is there a time after which a first draft stops being useful, or is later this week still the right reading of "early"?

grok-4.7/10-general-operations/WEEK_PLAN.md

Week plan — Monday to Friday

Hours are 09:30–17:30, Monday to Friday. Wednesday 13:00–17:30 is closed. Nothing in this plan sits outside those hours. Lunch is 45 minutes every day. Meeting load stays under the four-hour daily preference. Where someone else's availability was not stated, the time is marked as a hold or an ask, not as a booking.

What wins when two things want the same hour

  1. Board numbers, once Lina's corrections are in hand, through the Thursday 12:00 final.
  2. The renewals escalation, answered on Monday. It has no later day.
  3. Security-patch review on Monday, or on Tuesday if Monday cannot hold it, then QA and a 30-minute change window in time for a Friday 16:00 deploy.
  4. The other two support escalations, both done by Wednesday 12:15 (the last open block before lunch and the Wednesday afternoon close).
  5. Demo rehearsal with Sam, Wednesday 10:00–11:30, unless the board block has no earlier home.
  6. Strategy outline sent by Tuesday 14:00, so the 16:00 leadership meeting can review it.
  7. Customer demo hold, Thursday 16:00–17:30.

The strategy draft and the live demo move before the hard deadlines. The rehearsal moves only when the board block has no earlier home.

Protected, in the default week:

Dependencies

  1. Lina's Monday 15:00 update decides where the board block lives. The two hours cannot start before her corrections arrive.
  2. Board path: corrections, then two uninterrupted hours, then numbers final Thursday 12:00. Finance described those two hours as the work after a Tuesday-morning delivery.
  3. Patch path: review (60–90 min), then QA for half a day, then a 30-minute change window, deployed by Friday 16:00. QA's half day has to follow the review. QA cannot do Thursday.
  4. The review is planned for Monday, with Tuesday as the backup. Wednesday's default morning is the rehearsal plus two short gaps, and the board-slip morning is the two-hour finance block, so neither leaves 60–90 minutes. A Friday review is not in the plan: QA would have to start as soon as the review ended, and a half day plus the 30-minute window would have to finish by 16:00 with almost no slack. QA's own hours were not stated, so that back-to-back Friday is not something to count on.
  5. Strategy path: three focused hours, then a send by Tuesday 14:00, then an optional review in the Tuesday 16:00 meeting. Two of those hours have to happen Monday afternoon. Tuesday morning before 11:00 is only 90 minutes, and from 11:00 the default week belongs to the board numbers. Monday afternoon is what makes the Tuesday review possible.
  6. Rehearsal path: Sam is out Tuesday. His only stated window is Wednesday 10:00–12:00. The rehearsal is 90 minutes and he must join, so it is Wednesday 10:00–11:30.
  7. Demo path: Wednesday 15:00 is inside the closed afternoon. The remaining offered slot is Thursday 16:00. That hold still depends on the customer confirming, on the length fitting before 17:30, and on Sam if he is required live. His Thursday availability was not stated.
  8. Support path: the renewals item is Monday-only. Wednesday can absorb one later escalation (11:30–12:15) and not two. One Wednesday-SLA item is on Monday, the other on Tuesday, with Wednesday as the backup for the Tuesday one.

muse/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Response — reasoning behind the top three priorities

Priority 1: Start the security patch chain Monday (review → QA → deploy by Friday 16:00)

Reasoning: this is the week's longest dependency chain with the least slack. It requires three sequential steps — your 60–90 minute review, a QA half day, and a 30-minute change window — before a hard Friday 16:00 deploy, and one full day (Thursday) is removed because QA is unavailable. Any delay to the review compresses QA into Wednesday morning (when you are only available until 13:00 and already owe Sam a rehearsal) or Friday morning (hours before the deploy). Doing the review Monday is the only move that gives QA two normal options (Tuesday, Wednesday) plus a Friday fallback. It outranks even the board numbers because its chain is longer and its blackout day is absolute, whereas the board work has a Thursday-morning buffer after the Tuesday block.

Priority 2: Protect the board-numbers path (Lina checkpoint Monday 15:00, 2-hour block Tuesday, final Thursday 12:00)

Reasoning: Thursday 12:00 is a hard Finance deadline fed by an external dependency you do not control (Lina's corrections, themselves conditional on a vendor export). The Monday 15:00 checkpoint is the week's key early-warning signal: it tells you whether Tuesday 11:00–13:00 stays a board-numbers block or must be repurposed while a replacement block is found. Protecting two uninterrupted hours immediately after corrections is a stated Finance requirement, not a preference, so Tuesday late morning is non-negotiable once corrections land. This ranks below the security review only because its chain is shorter (one handoff, one block, one buffer) — but it outranks the demo, strategy draft, and non-renewal support items because its deadline is hard and its input is uncertain.

Priority 3: Resolve the demo slot and rehearsal by Monday (propose Thursday 16:00, lock Wednesday 10:00–11:30 with Sam)

Reasoning: the demo is the week's sharpest outright contradiction — the preferred Wednesday 15:00 slot falls inside your hard Wednesday PM unavailability, so it cannot happen as stated. Unlike other items, this one involves an external party (the customer) plus a constrained internal one (Sam, unavailable Tuesday, free only Wednesday 10:00–12:00), and it needs a 90-minute rehearsal inside your 3.5-hour Wednesday morning window. Every day of delay in confirming Thursday 16:00 and the Wednesday rehearsal risks losing Sam's window and the customer's fallback with no third option on record. It ranks third rather than first because it is still partly a preference (the customer offered two times) rather than a hard regulatory-style deadline — but it must be decided Monday, ahead of the strategy draft polish and the Wednesday support SLAs, because its calendar constraints are the tightest and involve other people's availability.

What was deliberately deprioritized

  • The CEO strategy outline ("early this week" / Tuesday 14:00 review gate): treated as an opportunity window, not a hard deadline, and scheduled in Monday focus time so it does not compete with Tuesday's protected board-numbers block. If forced to choose, hard deadlines win.
  • Non-renewal support escalations (Wednesday SLAs): front-loaded to Tuesday to respect the Wednesday 13:00 effective cutoff, but each is a bounded 45-minute task with no downstream chain, so they do not displace the three chain- and calendar-risk priorities above. The renewal escalation is an exception — it is Monday-urgent and handled first thing Monday, but it is a single small task rather than a week-shaping priority.

muse/10-general-operations/DECISIONS.md

Decisions

Confirmed decisions (derived from stated constraints)

  1. Customer demo moves to Thursday 16:00 (pending customer confirmation). Reason: the preferred Wednesday 15:00 falls inside your hard unavailability (Wednesday 13:00–17:30). No alternative interpretation makes Wednesday 15:00 work without inventing availability.
  2. Demo rehearsal is Wednesday 10:00–11:30 with Sam. Reason: Sam is unavailable Tuesday and available Wednesday 10:00–12:00; your only Wednesday availability is 09:30–13:00. This is the sole overlap for a 90-minute joint session.
  3. Security review happens Monday (90-minute block). Reason: the review → QA half day → change window → Friday 16:00 chain cannot pass through Thursday (QA unavailable), so the review must start Monday to leave Tuesday/Wednesday for QA and preserve Friday morning as fallback.
  4. Board-numbers work gets a protected 2-hour block Tuesday 11:00–13:00, contingent on Lina delivering by 11:00. Reason: Finance requires two uninterrupted hours after corrections; Thursday 12:00 is hard.
  5. Renewal-affecting escalation is answered Monday. Reason: explicitly required Monday; all other support items have Wednesday SLAs.
  6. Strategy draft gets 3 focused hours Monday to allow a Tuesday 14:00 send. Reason: this is the only way to make the Tuesday 16:00 leadership review without colliding with Tuesday's board-numbers block.

Unresolved contradictions

  1. Finance expects Tuesday-morning corrections → 2-hour block, but Lina conditions Tuesday 11:00 delivery on the vendor export (known Monday 15:00). If the export is late, the Tuesday block and possibly the Thursday 12:00 deadline are at risk. No late-delivery ETA exists yet.
  2. "Board numbers must be final by Thursday 12:00" leaves unclear what "final" includes (your 2 hours only, or also Finance-side processing?). If Finance needs time after your block, the real internal deadline is earlier than Thursday 12:00.
  3. Demo preference (Wednesday 15:00) contradicts your unavailability and leaves only one stated fallback (Thursday 16:00). If the customer cannot do Thursday, there is no third option in the notes.
  4. Security chain vs Thursday blackout: "QA half day after review" is underspecified (which half day, how many hours, does it need you present?). Thursday's unavailability for QA plus the Friday 16:00 deploy leaves no slack if review or QA slips past Wednesday.
  5. "Strategy outline early this week" vs the Tuesday 14:00 review gate: it is unclear whether Tuesday 14:00 is a hard expectation from the CEO or just an opportunity to use the leadership meeting.
  6. Wednesday SLAs vs Wednesday PM unavailability: the two non-renewal escalations are effectively due by Wednesday 13:00 for you, not end of Wednesday. The notes do not give exact SLA times.
  7. Wednesday morning overload: rehearsal (90 min) + lunch (45 min) inside a 3.5-hour availability window leaves about 75 minutes for everything else, including any SLA verification.

Questions, assigned

  • To Lina: by Monday 15:00, is the vendor export on track for Tuesday 11:00 corrections? If late, what is the new ETA and how will corrections arrive?
  • To Finance: what does "final by Thursday 12:00" require from you vs from Finance — is there Finance processing time after your 2 hours? Can the 2-hour block be split if corrections arrive late, or must it be uninterrupted? Who is the fallback if corrections slip to Wednesday?
  • To Product / customer: can you confirm Thursday 16:00 for the demo, and the expected duration and attendees? (Wednesday 15:00 is not possible — unavailable Wednesday afternoon.)
  • To Sam: please confirm rehearsal Wednesday 10:00–11:30 and what you need from the demo environment beforehand.
  • To Engineering / QA: please confirm the QA half-day slot (proposing Tuesday after Monday review, fallback Wednesday AM or Friday AM — Thursday excluded), how many hours "half day" means, whether you need to be present, and the booked 30-minute change-window time before Friday 16:00.
  • To CEO: is Tuesday 14:00 a required send time for the strategy outline, or is "early this week" flexible if Tuesday's board-numbers block takes priority? What decision do you need from the Tuesday 16:00 review?
  • To Support: which escalation affects renewals, and what are the exact Wednesday SLA times (cutoff hour)? Are 45-minute estimates per escalation inclusive of follow-up and documentation?

muse/10-general-operations/WEEK_PLAN.md

Week Plan — Monday to Friday

Working hours: 09:30–17:30, Monday–Friday. Unavailable Wednesday 13:00–17:30. All days reserve at least 45 minutes for lunch. Target: no more than 4 hours of meetings per day (focus work does not count as meetings).

Hard deadlines vs preferences

Hard deadlines (must not slip):

  • Monday (during work hours): respond to renewal-affecting escalation.
  • Wednesday (SLA, assumed end of work hours — confirm exact time): two remaining escalations due. Must be finished by Wednesday 13:00 given PM unavailability, so effectively due Tuesday/Wednesday morning.
  • Thursday 12:00: board numbers final.
  • Friday 16:00: security patch deployed.

Preferences / opportunity windows (important but not hard):

  • Customer demo preferred Wednesday 15:00, alternative Thursday 16:00. Wednesday 15:00 is impossible (you are unavailable). Plan assumes Thursday 16:00 pending customer confirmation.
  • CEO strategy outline "early this week"; Tuesday 14:00 send enables review at the Tuesday 16:00 leadership meeting. Missing 14:00 loses that review slot but is not a stated hard deadline.
  • Sam can rehearse Wednesday 10:00–12:00; unavailable Tuesday.
  • QA unavailable Thursday (constraint, not a deadline).

Dependencies

  1. Lina corrections (Tuesday by 11:00, unless vendor export late; known by Monday 15:00) → your 2-hour uninterrupted board-numbers block → Thursday 12:00 final.
  2. Your security review (60–90 min) → QA half day → 30-minute change window → Friday 16:00 deploy. QA cannot run Thursday, so review must finish early enough to give QA a half day on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Friday morning.
  3. Sam + rehearsal (90 min, Wednesday 10:00–12:00 window) → customer demo (Thursday 16:00 proposed).
  4. Strategy draft (3 focused hours) → optional Tuesday 14:00 send → Tuesday 16:00 leadership review.
  5. Renewal escalation → Monday response blocks nothing else but is the day's most urgent single item.

Monday

  • 09:30–10:00 — Triage. Confirm: renewal escalation identity, QA half-day booking, Sam rehearsal invite, Thursday demo proposal, Lina Monday 15:00 checkpoint.
  • 10:00–10:30 — Recurring meeting.
  • 10:30–11:15 — Support: renewal-affecting escalation (45 min). Hard deadline: today.
  • 11:15–12:45 — Security patch review (90 min, upper estimate). Unblocks QA.

  • 11:00–13:00 — Board numbers: 2 uninterrupted hours after Lina corrections (assumes on-time 11:00 arrival). Do not schedule over this.

kimi-k3/10-general-operations/RESPONSE.md

Response — reasoning behind my top three priorities

1. Board numbers (hard deadline: Thursday 12:00)

This is the week's most fragile hard deadline. It is immovable, it is for the board, and — critically — it depends on inputs I do not control: Lina's corrections, which in turn depend on a vendor export whose status is only known Monday 15:00. That combination (hard deadline + external dependency + a required two-hour uninterrupted block) means the priority is not just doing the work but building an early-warning system around it: the Monday 15:00 check-in with Lina, a protected Tuesday 11:00–13:00 block, and a pre-identified fallback (Thursday 10:00–12:00) with an explicit instruction to warn Finance the moment the fallback path triggers. Missing this deadline is not recoverable within the week, so it outranks everything else.

2. Security patch (hard deadline: Friday 16:00)

This has the longest dependency chain of the week — my 60–90 minute review → a half-day of QA → a 30-minute change window — and a hidden constraint: QA is unavailable Thursday, which quietly removes a day from what looks like a comfortable five-day runway. Starting my review Monday keeps two viable paths open (QA Tuesday + change window Wednesday; or, as a last resort, QA Friday morning + window before 16:00). Slipping the review by even one day collapses that slack and forces the tight Friday path as the only option. Security work also carries risk beyond the deadline itself, so it gets scheduled early and finished Wednesday.

3. Renewal-affecting support escalation (response needed Monday)

This is the smallest item (~45 minutes) but the only one with a Monday commitment, and it touches renewals — direct revenue risk. The cost of doing it is trivial; the cost of missing it is disproportionate and immediate. Clearing it first thing Monday (10:30–11:15, right after the recurring meeting) also protects the rest of the week's focus blocks from interruption. The other two escalations have Wednesday SLAs and are safely scheduled Tuesday afternoon with Wednesday morning as overflow.

Why the CEO's strategy outline is not in the top three

"Early this week" is a stated preference, not a hard deadline, and the Tuesday 16:00 leadership review is an opportunity with a graceful fallback (send later in the week). It still receives a protected three-hour focus block on Monday afternoon and a realistic shot at the Tuesday 14:00 send — but if anything above slips, the outline is the item that yields, because it is the only major task whose deadline can move without breaking a commitment to an external party.
